<span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/docs/en/skills">Skills</a> and <a class="link" href="/docs/en/commands">commands</a> inject their instructions as user messages at the point of invocation. Nothing earlier in the conversation changes. A skill or command whose frontmatter names a <code>model</code> can be a <a href="#switching-models" class="link">model switch</a> for that turn.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="running-/recap"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#running-/recap" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Running <code>/recap</code></span></h3>
<span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/docs/en/interactive-mode#session-recap"><code>/recap</code></a> generates a summary for display in your terminal. Unlike <code>/compact</code>, it appends the summary as command output rather than replacing your message history, so the cached prefix stays intact.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="rewinding-the-conversation"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#rewinding-the-conversation" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Rewinding the conversation</span></h3>
<span data-as="p"><a class="link" href="/docs/en/checkpointing"><code>/rewind</code></a> truncates your conversation back to an earlier turn. The remaining history is the same content the cache was built from at that point, and the system prompt and project context layers are unchanged, so the next request hits the earlier cache entry. Every turn since then has read through that prefix, which kept the entry warm even if the original turn was longer ago than the TTL.</span>
<span data-as="p">Restoring file checkpoints alongside the conversation has no separate effect on the cache. File contents enter context only when Claude reads them, the same as <a href="#editing-files-in-your-repository" class="link">editing files in your repository</a>.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="resuming-a-session"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#resuming-a-session" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Resuming a session</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">When you <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sessions#resume-a-session">resume a session</a>, Claude Code sends the whole conversation again, and the request reads from the cache whatever part of its prefix is unchanged and still within the <a href="#cache-lifetime" class="link">cache lifetime</a>. The layer table at the top of this page says what changes each layer.</span>
<span data-as="p">The system prompt would change after a <a href="#upgrading-claude-code" class="link">Claude Code upgrade</a> or with different <a class="link" href="/docs/en/cli-reference#system-prompt-flags"><code>--append-system-prompt</code></a> text on the resume. By default, the resumed conversation keeps the system prompt it started with, so its history still sits behind the same prompt, and the change takes effect once the conversation is compacted or in a new conversation. <a class="link" href="/docs/en/cli-reference#system-prompt-flags-in-resumed-conversations">System prompt flags in resumed conversations</a> covers the cases where Claude Code rebuilds the prompt on every request instead.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="cache-lifetime"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#cache-lifetime" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Cache lifetime</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Cached prefixes expire after a period of inactivity. Each request that hits the cache resets the timer, so the cache stays warm as long as you keep working. After a long enough gap, the next request recomputes the full input and re-establishes the cache, which is why the first turn back after stepping away can be noticeably slower.</span>
<span data-as="p">On a Pro or Max plan, when you resume a large session after a long break, Claude Code <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sessions#resume-from-a-summary">offers to resume from a summary</a> so later requests don’t carry the full history.</span>
<span data-as="p">The time to live (TTL) controls how long a gap the cache survives. The API offers two: a five-minute TTL, and a <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#1-hour-cache-duration" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">one-hour TTL</a> that keeps the cache warm through longer breaks but <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#pricing" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">bills cache writes at a higher rate</a>. The longer TTL helps when you leave a session idle and come back to it, because you skip the reprocessing an expired prefix costs. It costs more on short bursts of work that never idle past five minutes, where the higher write rate applies and the longer cache lifetime goes unused.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="which-ttl-each-request-gets"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#which-ttl-each-request-gets" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Which TTL each request gets</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">Claude Code decides the TTL per request, and every request falls in one of two fixed buckets:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Main conversation</strong>: your interactive turns, non-interactive <code>-p</code> runs, and Agent SDK turns, plus the helpers Claude Code runs inline with them</li>
<li><strong>Everything else</strong>: the requests Claude Code makes outside that conversation, such as <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sub-agents">subagents</a>, <a class="link" href="/docs/en/workflows">workflows</a>, in-process <a class="link" href="/docs/en/agent-teams">teammates</a>, forks, compaction, and session titles</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Unless you choose a TTL yourself, Claude Code requests the one-hour TTL only on a Claude subscription within your plan’s included usage. There it requests the hour for the main conversation, plus a small set of helper requests that Anthropic controls server-side. This table gives each bucket’s default TTL under both kinds of billing.</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div role="region" data-id="base-ui-_R_rcllktbsnlhjiuqsnpfiutb_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Request bucket</th><th>Claude subscription, within plan usage</th><th>Usage credits, API key, or cloud provider</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Main conversation</td><td>One hour</td><td>Five minutes</td></tr><tr><td>Everything else</td><td>Five minutes, except the server-controlled helper requests, which get one hour</td><td>Five minutes</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">Once you go over your plan’s usage limit and Claude Code draws on <a href="https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">usage credits</a>, you are billed for that usage, so Claude Code drops the main conversation to the cheaper five-minute TTL. To keep the one-hour TTL there, <a href="#choose-the-ttl-yourself" class="link">choose the TTL yourself</a>.</span>
<h3 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="choose-the-ttl-yourself"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#choose-the-ttl-yourself" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Choose the TTL yourself</span></h3>
<span data-as="p">You can set a TTL for either bucket. Each control takes <code>5m</code> or <code>1h</code>, and Claude Code ignores any other value.</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Main conversation</strong>: the <a class="link" href="/docs/en/settings-reference#promptcachettl"><code>promptCacheTtl</code></a> setting, or the <code>CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL</code> <a class="link" href="/docs/en/env-vars">environment variable</a></li>
<li><strong>Everything else</strong>: the <a class="link" href="/docs/en/settings-reference#subagentpromptcachettl"><code>subagentPromptCacheTtl</code></a> setting, or the <code>CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL</code> environment variable</li>
</ul>
<span data-as="p">Both settings and both environment variables require Claude Code v2.1.242 or later. If you sign in with an API key or use a cloud provider, set <code>promptCacheTtl</code> to <code>1h</code> to give the main conversation a one-hour cache. Requests outside it keep the five-minute default until you choose a TTL for that bucket too.</span>
<span data-as="p">When more than one control applies, Claude Code takes the first match in this order:</span>
<ol>
<li><code>FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M=1</code>, which forces five minutes for both buckets</li>
<li>The bucket’s environment variable</li>
<li>The bucket’s setting</li>
<li>For a subagent’s requests, the <code>cacheTtl</code> value in the subagent’s <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sub-agents#supported-frontmatter-fields"><code>experimental</code> frontmatter field</a>, which requires Claude Code v2.1.248 or later. Claude Code ignores a <code>1h</code> there while your Claude subscription is using usage credits</li>
<li><code>ENABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_1H=1</code>, which requests one hour for both buckets</li>
<li>The <a href="#which-ttl-each-request-gets" class="link">default for the request’s bucket</a></li>
</ol>
<span data-as="p">Set <code>FORCE_PROMPT_CACHING_5M=1</code> when you’re debugging cache behavior, comparing the two TTLs, or overriding a longer TTL set in <a class="link" href="/docs/en/managed-settings">managed settings</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">To confirm which TTL your main conversation’s cache writes used, run <code>claude -p &quot;hello&quot; --output-format json</code> and read <code>usage.cache_creation</code> in the result. Claude Code reports one-hour cache writes under <code>ephemeral_1h_input_tokens</code> and five-minute cache writes under <code>ephemeral_5m_input_tokens</code>.</span>
<span data-as="p">Through an LLM gateway you set with <code>ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL</code>, part of the one-hour request travels in the <code>anthropic-beta</code> header, so configure the gateway to <a class="link" href="/docs/en/llm-gateway-protocol#request-headers">forward that header unchanged</a>. The one-hour TTL isn’t available through the <a class="link" href="/docs/en/claude-apps-gateway#availability-and-limitations">Claude apps gateway</a>. On Amazon Bedrock, prompt caching support, minimum cacheable prefix length, and one-hour TTL availability all vary by model. If cache token counts stay at zero, check <a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html#prompt-caching-models" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">supported models, regions, and limits</a> in the Amazon Bedrock documentation.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="cache-scope"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#cache-scope" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Cache scope</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">In Claude Code, the cache is effectively scoped to one machine and directory. Each conversation carries the working directory, platform, shell, and OS version, and the system prompt names your auto memory paths, so two sessions in different directories build different prefixes and miss each other’s cache. That includes worktrees of the same repository, since each worktree has its own working directory.</span>
<span data-as="p">Sessions you run in parallel in the same directory build matching prefixes and read each other’s cache. Sequential sessions share the prefix only when the git status snapshot taken at startup matches, since each conversation also carries the branch and recent commits from that snapshot.</span>
<span data-as="p">The underlying API cache is broader. Caches are isolated between organizations, and on some providers, <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#cache-storage-and-sharing" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">between workspaces within an organization</a>. Within those boundaries, any two requests with the same model and prefix read the same cache. For Agent SDK callers running fleets of automated processes, see <a class="link" href="/docs/en/agent-sdk/modifying-system-prompts#improve-prompt-caching-across-users-and-machines">improve prompt caching across users and machines</a> to suppress the per-machine sections of the system prompt and share the cache across machines.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="check-cache-performance"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#check-cache-performance" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Check cache performance</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Cache performance shows up as two token counts the API reports on every response. The most direct way to watch them live is a <a class="link" href="/docs/en/statusline">statusline script</a> that reads the <code>current_usage</code> object:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div role="region" data-id="base-ui-_R_vkllktbsnlhjiuqsnpfiutb_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Field</th><th>Meaning</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>cache_creation_input_tokens</code></td><td>Tokens written to the cache on this turn, billed at the cache write rate</td></tr><tr><td><code>cache_read_input_tokens</code></td><td>Tokens served from cache on this turn, billed at the model’s <a href="https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer" class="link">cached token rate</a>, below the standard input rate</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">A high read-to-creation ratio means caching is working well. If creation stays high turn after turn, something is changing in your prefix. The <a href="#actions-that-invalidate-the-cache" class="link">actions that invalidate the cache</a> section lists the usual causes.</span>
<span data-as="p">For a per-session summary, run <code>/usage</code>. After the main conversation’s first response, Claude Code adds a <a class="link" href="/docs/en/costs#prompt-cache-statistics"><code>Prompt cache (main)</code> line</a> to the Session block, showing the session’s hit ratio, miss count, and whether the cache is warm right now. A status line script can read the same numbers from the <a class="link" href="/docs/en/statusline#prompt-cache-fields"><code>prompt_cache</code> object</a>. Both require Claude Code v2.1.251 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">The <code>Prompt cache (main)</code> line also names the likely cause of the last miss when Claude Code can identify one, for example <code>likely cause: tool definitions changed</code>. The likely-cause text requires Claude Code v2.1.260 or later.</span>
<span data-as="p">For visibility across an organization, the OpenTelemetry exporter reports cache read and creation tokens per user and session. See <a class="link" href="/docs/en/monitoring-usage">Monitor usage</a> for the metric and event attribute reference.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="subagents-and-the-cache"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#subagents-and-the-cache" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Subagents and the cache</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">A <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sub-agents">subagent</a> starts its own conversation with its own system prompt and tool set, separate from the parent’s. Its first request doesn’t read the parent’s cache, because the two prefixes differ, and it warms a cache of its own across its turns. Subagents fall outside the main-conversation <a href="#which-ttl-each-request-gets" class="link">TTL bucket</a>, so they get five minutes even on a subscription until you <a href="#choose-the-ttl-yourself" class="link">choose a longer one</a>.</span>
<span data-as="p">The parent’s cache is unaffected. From the parent’s side, the subagent’s call and result append to the conversation, leaving the parent’s prefix intact.</span>
<span data-as="p">A <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sub-agents#fork-the-current-conversation">fork</a>, by contrast, inherits the parent’s system prompt, tools, and conversation history exactly, so its first request reads the parent’s cache.</span>
<span data-as="p">Other requests can also read a prefix that an earlier request cached:</span>
<ul>
<li><strong>Session copies</strong>: a session you <a class="link" href="/docs/en/agent-view#copy-the-session-with-%2Ffork">copy with <code>/fork</code></a> receives its isolation instruction as a message at the end of the copied conversation, so the cache that the original conversation built stays intact.</li>
<li><strong>Compaction</strong>: the summarization call described in <a href="#compacting-the-conversation" class="link">Compacting the conversation</a> uses the same prefix-sharing approach.</li>
<li><strong>Resumed subagents</strong>: when Claude <a class="link" href="/docs/en/sub-agents#resume-subagents">resumes a subagent</a>, the resumed run’s first request can read the cache the original run warmed.</li>
<li><strong>Workflow fan-outs</strong>: in a <a class="link" href="/docs/en/workflows#prompt-caching-in-a-fan-out">workflow fan-out</a> of same-prefix agents, Claude Code holds all but the first for up to 5 seconds by default, so their first requests can read the prefix that the first agent cached.</li>
</ul>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="disable-prompt-caching"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#disable-prompt-caching" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Disable prompt caching</span></h2>
<span data-as="p">Disabling caching is occasionally useful when debugging caching behavior with a specific model or provider. To turn it off, set one of these environment variables to <code>1</code>:</span>
<!-- --><div role="presentation" style="position:relative;--scroll-area-corner-height:0px;--scroll-area-corner-width:0px" data-component-part="scroll-area" data-table-wrapper="true" class="min-w-0 [--page-padding:20px] flex w-[calc(100%+(var(--page-padding)*2))] my-[1em] py-[1em] -mx-(--page-padding) max-w-none [contain:inline-size]"><div role="region" data-id="base-ui-_R_12sllktbsnlhjiuqsnpfiutb_-viewport" tabindex="-1" class="size-full rounded-[inherit] [--scroll-area-fade-size:32px] overflow-y-hidden! base-ui-disable-scrollbar" style="overflow:scroll" data-component-part="scroll-area-viewport" aria-label="Scrollable table"><div role="presentation" style="min-width:fit-content" data-component-part="scroll-area-content" class="flex"><div class="px-(--page-padding) grow max-w-none table"><table class="m-0 min-w-full w-full max-w-none table [&amp;_th]:text-left [&amp;_td[data-numeric]]:tabular-nums [&amp;_td]:min-w-[150px]"><thead><tr><th>Variable</th><th>Effect</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td><code>DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING</code></td><td>Disable for all models</td></tr><tr><td><code>DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_HAIKU</code></td><td>Disable for Haiku only</td></tr><tr><td><code>DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_SONNET</code></td><td>Disable for Sonnet only</td></tr><tr><td><code>DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_OPUS</code></td><td>Disable for Opus only</td></tr><tr><td><code>DISABLE_PROMPT_CACHING_FABLE</code></td><td>Disable for Fable only</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div></div></div>
<span data-as="p">To set caching policy across an organization, put any of these or the <a href="#cache-lifetime" class="link">TTL variables</a> in the <code>env</code> block of <a class="link" href="/docs/en/managed-settings">managed settings</a>. For normal use, leave caching enabled.</span>
<h2 class="flex whitespace-pre-wrap group font-semibold" id="related-resources"><div class="absolute" tabindex="-1"><a href="#related-resources" class="-ml-10 flex items-center opacity-0 border-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100 focus:outline-0 group/link" aria-label="Navigate to header">​<div class="size-6 rounded-md flex items-center justify-center shadow-xs text-gray-400 dark:text-white/50 dark:bg-background-dark dark:brightness-[1.35] dark:ring-1 dark:hover:brightness-150 bg-white ring-1 ring-gray-400/30 dark:ring-gray-700/25 hover:ring-gray-400/60 dark:hover:ring-white/20 group-focus/link:border-2 group-focus/link:border-primary dark:group-focus/link:border-primary-light"><svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" width="18" height="18" viewBox="0 0 18 18" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" aria-hidden="true" class="size-3 shrink-0"><path d="M8.50001 6.827C8.14801 6.995 7.81801 7.225 7.52701 7.517L7.51701 7.527C6.13601 8.908 6.13601 11.146 7.51701 12.527L9.69201 14.702C11.073 16.083 13.311 16.083 14.692 14.702L14.702 14.692C16.083 13.311 16.083 11.073 14.702 9.692L13.771 8.761" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path><path d="M9.50002 11.173C9.85202 11.005 10.182 10.775 10.473 10.483L10.483 10.473C11.864 9.092 11.864 6.854 10.483 5.473L8.30802 3.298C6.92702 1.917 4.68902 1.917 3.30802 3.298L3.29802 3.308C1.91702 4.689 1.91702 6.927 3.29802 8.308L4.22902 9.239" stroke="currentColor" width="1.5" linecap="round" linejoin="round"></path></svg></div></a></div><span class="cursor-pointer">Related resources</span></h2>

_jsxs(_Fragment,{children:[_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Prompt caching makes Claude Code faster and more cost-efficient. Without caching, the API would reprocess your full history on every turn. With caching, it reuses what it already processed, bills the re-read at the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/pricing`,children:`cached token rate`}),`, and fully processes only what changed.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Claude Code handles prompt caching for you, unless you `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#disable-prompt-caching`,children:`disable it`}),`. It is still useful to know how prompt caching works, because some actions invalidate the cache and make the next response slower and more expensive while it rebuilds. This page covers which actions those are, why some settings wait for a restart to apply, and how to check cache performance when usage looks high.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`how-the-cache-is-organized`,children:`How the cache is organized`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Each time you send a message in Claude Code, it makes a new API request. The model doesn’t remember anything between requests, so Claude Code re-sends the full context: the system prompt, your project context, every prior message and tool result, and your new message. New content is appended at the end, which means most of each request is identical to the one before it. Prompt caching is how the API avoids reprocessing the part that didn’t change.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The API caches by matching the start of each request, called the prefix, against content it recently processed. On a normal turn, the prefix is the entire previous request and only the latest exchange is new. The match is exact, so a change anywhere in the prefix recomputes everything after it. There is no per-file or per-segment caching. See `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#how-prompt-caching-works`,children:`how prompt caching works`}),` in the API reference for the underlying mechanism.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(OptimizedImage,{src:`https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=f2e8f0b8298a50305fe428ca3f1d1594`,className:`dark:mint-hidden`,alt:`Four turns shown as growing horizontal bars. Each turn's request contains everything from the previous turn plus the latest exchange appended at the end. On turns two and three, the unchanged prefix is read from cache and only the new exchange is processed. On turn four, the system prompt changed, so the prefix no longer matches and the entire request is reprocessed and written.`,\"data-og-width\":`720`,width:`720`,\"data-og-height\":`454`,height:`454`,\"data-path\":`images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg`,\"data-optimize\":`true`,\"data-opv\":`3`,srcset:`https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=280\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=f80740cf93341348514361d1fdc8b03d 280w, https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=560\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=9f28222370c20cfce6fef1df4c5f4681 560w, https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=840\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=e01af43d858af1e744a993259b842a40 840w, https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=1100\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=4478e371efd228755a9260811c9584a8 1100w, https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=1650\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=c983942419ed352d1b45be07f69f2777 1650w, https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm/images/prompt-caching-prefix.svg?w=2500\u0026fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=VbDJw--l6T9a9Wvm\u0026q=85\u0026s=04947fe0366ad82726d0b1494e2e2b3e 2500w`}),`\n`,_jsx(OptimizedImage,{src:`https://mintcdn.com/claude-code/_xqph1dUOslCOwsj/images/prompt-caching-prefix-dark.svg?fit=max\u0026auto=format\u0026n=_xqph1dUOslCOwsj\u0026q=85\u0026s=297dc1c639f0915cae858d0c4b6f3be5`,className:`mint-hidden dark:mint-block`,alt:`Four turns shown as growing horizontal bars. Switching with `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/model-config#setting-your-model`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/model`})}),` means the next request reads the entire conversation history with no cache hits, even though the content is identical.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When you run `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/model`}),` at the terminal, Claude Code asks you to confirm the switch only while the cache is still warm and the new model isn’t the one that produced the last response. The cache stays warm for one `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#cache-lifetime`,children:`cache TTL`}),` after Claude Code last sent a request in this conversation or Claude last responded. Once that time passes, the cache has expired, so Claude Code switches without asking.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Before v2.1.238, Claude Code didn’t check the cache TTL and asked even after the cache had expired.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`You can also require this confirmation or skip it with a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/hooks#premodelswitch-decision-control`,children:`PreModelSwitch hook`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/model-config#opusplan-model-setting`,children:[_jsx(_components.code,{children:`opusplan`}),` model setting`]}),` resolves to Opus during plan mode and Sonnet during execution, so each plan-mode toggle is a model switch and starts a fresh cache.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/model-config#automatic-model-fallback`,children:`Automatic model fallback`}),` on Fable models, Opus 5.5, and Opus 5 is also a model switch. The session model resumes on your next prompt. A `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`context: fork`}),` skill sets the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/skills#run-skills-in-a-subagent`,children:`forked subagent’s model`}),` instead.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`changing-effort-level`,children:`Changing effort level`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`On most models, changing the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/model-config#adjust-effort-level`,children:`effort level`}),` mid-session means the next request reads the entire conversation history with no cache hits. While the cache is still warm, Claude Code asks you to confirm the change first.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`On Opus 5.5 and Fable 5.1 with an API key or a Claude subscription, changing effort keeps the cache, and Claude Code applies the new level without asking. This doesn’t apply on Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud’s Agent Platform, or a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/claude-apps-gateway`,children:`Claude apps gateway`}),`, or when you set `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/llm-gateway-protocol#disable-pre-release-capabilities`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS`})}),` or your organization has a HIPAA configuration.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Before v2.1.260, changing effort on Fable 5.1 with an API key or a Claude subscription also invalidated the cache.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`turning-on-fast-mode`,children:`Turning on fast mode`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Enabling `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/fast-mode`,children:`fast mode`}),` adds a request header that is part of the cache key, so the first request Claude Code sends with fast mode on reads the entire conversation history with no cache hits. Claude Code sets that header once when a turn starts and keeps it for the whole turn, so when you turn fast mode on while Claude is working, the cache miss from the header happens on the first request of your next turn. Those uncached input tokens are billed at `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/fast-mode#understand-the-cost-tradeoff`,children:`fast mode rates`}),`, which is why turning it on at the start of a session costs less than turning it on deep into a long one. If your current model doesn’t support fast mode, enabling fast mode also `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#switching-models`,children:`switches your model`}),`, and that switch starts a fresh cache on its own from the next request in the running turn.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The cost applies once per conversation. After the first fast mode turn, Claude Code keeps sending the header and varies only the request’s speed setting, which is not part of the cache key. Turning fast mode off, the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/fast-mode#handle-rate-limits`,children:`automatic fallback to standard speed`}),` after a rate limit, and turning it back on later all keep the cache. If you `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/fast-mode#handle-rate-limits`,children:`run out of usage credits`}),` mid-session, Claude Code retries each rejected fast mode request at standard speed the same way, so this fallback also keeps the cache. `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/clear`}),` and `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),` reset this, since they rebuild the cache at those points anyway.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`connecting-or-disconnecting-an-mcp-server`,children:`Connecting or disconnecting an MCP server`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Tool definitions sit in the system prompt layer, so the cache invalidates when the set of tool definitions in the request changes between turns. Toggling the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/advisor`,children:`advisor tool`}),` is an exception: its definition sits after the cache breakpoint, so enabling or disabling `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/advisor`}),` keeps the cached prefix intact. Whether an `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp`,children:`MCP server`}),` change does this depends on whether its tools are deferred by `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#scale-with-mcp-tool-search`,children:`tool search`}),` or loaded into the prefix:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Deferred tools`}),`, the default on supported models: a server connecting, disconnecting, or changing its tool list only appends new content and doesn’t disturb anything already cached.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Tools loaded into the prefix`}),`: any change to them invalidates the cache. This happens when `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#configure-tool-search`,children:`tool search is unavailable or disabled`}),`, such as on Google Cloud’s Agent Platform models earlier than the Claude 4.5 generation, with a custom `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`}),` gateway, or on a Microsoft Foundry `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/claude-in-microsoft-foundry#hosting-options`,children:`deployment hosted on Azure`}),` once Claude Code detects that the deployment rejects tool search. It also happens for a server or tool marked `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#exempt-a-server-from-deferral`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`alwaysLoad`})}),`, and for definitions kept upfront by `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#configure-tool-search`,children:`threshold-based loading`}),`.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When tools load into the prefix, the most common cause of an invalidation is a server connecting or disconnecting mid-session, which can happen without any action on your part: a stdio server’s process exits, an HTTP session expires, or a server `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#automatic-reconnection`,children:`reconnects automatically after a transient failure`}),`. A connected server can also push a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/mcp#dynamic-tool-updates`,children:`dynamic tool update`}),` that changes its tool list.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Editing your MCP config does not by itself change the cache. The new config takes effect only after a restart, which is when the server connects or disconnects.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`enabling-or-disabling-a-plugin`,children:`Enabling or disabling a plugin`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When you enable or disable a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/plugins`,children:`plugin`}),`, what the change costs depends on which component types the plugin provides. It appends their content after the existing conversation, so the next request pays for that content and still reads everything before it from the cache.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`plugins-that-provide-mcp-servers`,children:`Plugins that provide MCP servers`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When you enable or disable a plugin that provides `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/plugins-reference#mcp-servers`,children:`MCP servers`}),`, Claude Code follows the same rules as when you `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#connecting-or-disconnecting-an-mcp-server`,children:`connect or disconnect an MCP server`}),`:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`If Claude Code defers the server’s tools, it keeps the cache.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.li,{children:`If Claude Code loads them into the prefix, the next request re-reads the entire conversation.`}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`code-intelligence-plugins`,children:`Code intelligence plugins`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When you enable a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/discover-plugins#code-intelligence`,children:`code intelligence plugin`}),`, Claude gets the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/tools-reference#lsp-tool-behavior`,children:`LSP tool`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`4`,id:`when-plugin-changes-apply`,children:`When plugin changes apply`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`A change you make in the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/plugin`}),` menu goes through `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/discover-plugins#apply-plugin-changes-without-restarting`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/reload-plugins`})}),`, which Claude Code runs for you when you close the menu. You pay the cost, whether appended announcements or a full re-read, on the first turn after the change applies. Claude Code can also apply a change on its own:`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`For a plugin with a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`command`}),` source, Claude Code `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/plugin-marketplaces#when-claude-code-re-runs-the-command`,children:`can reload the plugin itself`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`When you `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/discover-plugins#install-plugins`,children:[`install a plugin from the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/plugin`}),` interface`]}),`, Claude Code can activate it during the install. The install summary tells you whether it did.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`When you `,_jsxs(_components.a,{href:`/en/permissions#move-the-session-to-another-directory`,children:[`move the session with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/cd`})]}),` on v2.1.246 or later, Claude Code applies the plugins the new directory’s settings enable as part of the move, without the full re-read warning that holds a `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/reload-plugins`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[`In interactive sessions, when you add or remove a plugin in a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/plugins#test-your-plugins-locally`,children:`folder of plugins`}),` you passed with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`--plugin-dir`}),`, the change applies right away. If applying it would trigger a full re-read, Claude Code holds the change instead and shows a notice to run `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/reload-plugins`}),`. Requires Claude Code v2.1.265 or later.`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`When `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/reload-plugins`}),` runs and the reload would trigger a full re-read, Claude Code shows a warning and doesn’t apply the reload. If that prefix is still within its `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#cache-lifetime`,children:`cache lifetime`}),`, the next request reads the older cache entry instead of rebuilding.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`denying-an-entire-tool`,children:`Denying an entire tool`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`If you add a bare tool name like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash`}),` or `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`WebFetch`}),` as a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/permissions#manage-permissions`,children:`deny rule`}),`, Claude can’t call that tool from your next request on, whether you add the rule through `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/permissions`}),` or by `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/settings#when-edits-take-effect`,children:`editing a settings file directly`}),`. When tool search is unavailable or disabled, Claude Code removes the definition from the next request, which invalidates the cache, and so does removing the rule later.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Only a deny rule that matches in the tool-name position blocks a tool this way: a bare tool name, the equivalent `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash(*)`}),` form, or a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/permissions#tool-name-wildcards`,children:`tool-name glob`}),` like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"*\"`}),`. A glob that matches only MCP tools, such as `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`\"mcp__*\"`}),`, blocks those tools the same way. Scoped deny rules like `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`Bash(rm *)`}),`, and all allow and ask rules, don’t change which tools Claude sees. Claude Code checks them when Claude attempts a call, leaving the prefix intact.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`compacting-the-conversation`,children:`Compacting the conversation`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/context-window#what-survives-compaction`,children:`Compaction`}),` replaces your message history with a summary. By design, this invalidates the conversation layer, since the next request has a new, shorter history that doesn’t share a prefix with the old one. Claude Code reuses the system prompt layer unless the conversation was `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#resuming-a-session`,children:`resumed while keeping a system prompt that would otherwise have changed`}),`; in that case the first compaction switches to the current prompt and that layer rebuilds once. It reloads project context from disk, which cache-hits only if CLAUDE.md and memory are unchanged since the session started.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`To produce the summary, Claude Code sends a separate request with the same system prompt, tools, and history as your conversation, plus a summarization instruction appended as a final user message. While the cache is warm, that request reads your prefix from the cache, so a mid-session `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),` costs a fraction of what the context size suggests and spends most of its time generating the summary.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`After a break longer than the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#cache-lifetime`,children:`cache lifetime`}),`, there is no cache left to read, so the summarization request reprocesses the full history as uncached input. This is why `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),` costs the most when you `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/sessions#resume-from-a-summary`,children:`resume an old session`}),`. In both the warm and cold cases, the turn after compaction rebuilds the conversation cache for only the much shorter summary, so that turn is not the slow part.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Tip,{children:_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Compaction works in your favor when the context you discard is content you no longer need. To choose when its overhead happens, run `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/compact`}),` at a natural break in your work, such as between tasks, instead of waiting for auto-compaction to trigger mid-task. If you’ve gone down a path you want to abandon entirely, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#rewinding-the-conversation`,children:_jsx(_components.code,{children:`/rewind`})}),` to an earlier turn instead. Rewinding truncates back to a prefix that is already cached, rather than building a new one as compaction does.`]})}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`accumulating-many-images`,children:`Accumulating many images`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The API limits how many images and PDFs each request can carry. For the current numbers, see `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/vision#request-limits`,children:`Request limits`}),` in the API docs. Claude Code also caps the total size of the images and PDFs in a request, so large screenshots reach the limit with fewer images than small ones.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`When the next request would pass either limit, Claude Code removes a batch of the oldest images and PDFs from what it sends, which leaves room for more before it needs to remove any again. Claude can no longer see the removed images. If Claude needs one of them again, share it again.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Removing images changes the messages that held them, so the next request reprocesses the conversation from the earliest of those messages onward. Because Claude Code removes a batch at a time, you see one slower turn per batch rather than one with each new screenshot.`}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`upgrading-claude-code`,children:`Upgrading Claude Code`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`A new Claude Code version typically updates the system prompt or tool definitions, so the first conversation you start after an upgrade builds its cache from the top. `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/setup#auto-updates`,children:`Auto-update`}),` downloads new versions in the background but applies them on the next launch, never mid-session, so you see this as an uncached first turn after restarting rather than a surprise during a session. Each request that hits the cache resets the timer, so the cache stays warm as long as you keep working. After a long enough gap, the next request recomputes the full input and re-establishes the cache, which is why the first turn back after stepping away can be noticeably slower.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`On a Pro or Max plan, when you resume a large session after a long break, Claude Code `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/sessions#resume-from-a-summary`,children:`offers to resume from a summary`}),` so later requests don’t carry the full history.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The time to live (TTL) controls how long a gap the cache survives. The API offers two: a five-minute TTL, and a `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#1-hour-cache-duration`,children:`one-hour TTL`}),` that keeps the cache warm through longer breaks but `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#pricing`,children:`bills cache writes at a higher rate`}),`. The longer TTL helps when you leave a session idle and come back to it, because you skip the reprocessing an expired prefix costs. It costs more on short bursts of work that never idle past five minutes, where the higher write rate applies and the longer cache lifetime goes unused.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`3`,id:`which-ttl-each-request-gets`,children:`Which TTL each request gets`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Claude Code decides the TTL per request, and every request falls in one of two fixed buckets:`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.ul,{children:[`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Main conversation`}),`: your interactive turns, non-interactive `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`-p`}),` runs, and Agent SDK turns, plus the helpers Claude Code runs inline with them`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.li,{children:[_jsx(_components.strong,{children:`Everything else`}),`: the requests Claude Code makes outside that conversation, such as `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/sub-agents`,children:`subagents`}),`, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/workflows`,children:`workflows`}),`, in-process `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-teams`,children:`teammates`}),`, forks, compaction, and session titles`]}),`\n`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Unless you choose a TTL yourself, Claude Code requests the one-hour TTL only on a Claude subscription within your plan’s included usage. There it requests the hour for the main conversation, plus a small set of helper requests that Anthropic controls server-side. This table gives each bucket’s default TTL under both kinds of billing.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(Table,{children:[_jsx(_components.thead,{children:_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.th,{children:`Request bucket`}),_jsx(_components.th,{children:`Claude subscription, within plan usage`}),_jsx(_components.th,{children:`Usage credits, API key, or cloud provider`})]})}),_jsxs(_components.tbody,{children:[_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{children:`Main conversation`}),_jsx(_components.td,{children:`One hour`}),_jsx(_components.td,{children:`Five minutes`})]}),_jsxs(_components.tr,{children:[_jsx(_components.td,{children:`Everything else`}),_jsx(_components.td,{children:`Five minutes, except the server-controlled helper requests, which get one hour`}),_jsx(_components.td,{children:`Five minutes`})]})]})]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Once you go over your plan’s usage limit and Claude Code draws on `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12429409-extra-usage-for-paid-claude-plans`,children:`usage credits`}),`, you are billed for that usage, so Claude Code drops the main conversation to the cheaper five-minute TTL. Claude Code reports one-hour cache writes under `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ephemeral_1h_input_tokens`}),` and five-minute cache writes under `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ephemeral_5m_input_tokens`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Through an LLM gateway you set with `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`}),`, part of the one-hour request travels in the `,_jsx(_components.code,{children:`anthropic-beta`}),` header, so configure the gateway to `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/llm-gateway-protocol#request-headers`,children:`forward that header unchanged`}),`. The one-hour TTL isn’t available through the `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/claude-apps-gateway#availability-and-limitations`,children:`Claude apps gateway`}),`. On Amazon Bedrock, prompt caching support, minimum cacheable prefix length, and one-hour TTL availability all vary by model. If cache token counts stay at zero, check `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/prompt-caching.html#prompt-caching-models`,children:`supported models, regions, and limits`}),` in the Amazon Bedrock documentation.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`cache-scope`,children:`Cache scope`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`In Claude Code, the cache is effectively scoped to one machine and directory. Each conversation carries the working directory, platform, shell, and OS version, and the system prompt names your auto memory paths, so two sessions in different directories build different prefixes and miss each other’s cache. That includes worktrees of the same repository, since each worktree has its own working directory.`}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`Sessions you run in parallel in the same directory build matching prefixes and read each other’s cache. Sequential sessions share the prefix only when the git status snapshot taken at startup matches, since each conversation also carries the branch and recent commits from that snapshot.`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`The underlying API cache is broader. Caches are isolated between organizations, and on some providers, `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/build-with-claude/prompt-caching#cache-storage-and-sharing`,children:`between workspaces within an organization`}),`. Within those boundaries, any two requests with the same model and prefix read the same cache. For Agent SDK callers running fleets of automated processes, see `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/agent-sdk/modifying-system-prompts#improve-prompt-caching-across-users-and-machines`,children:`improve prompt caching across users and machines`}),` to suppress the per-machine sections of the system prompt and share the cache across machines.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(Heading,{level:`2`,id:`check-cache-performance`,children:`Check cache performance`}),`\n`,_jsxs(_components.p,{children:[`Cache performance shows up as two token counts the API reports on every response. Subagents fall outside the main-conversation `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#which-ttl-each-request-gets`,children:`TTL bucket`}),`, so they get five minutes even on a subscription until you `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`#choose-the-ttl-yourself`,children:`choose a longer one`}),`.`]}),`\n`,_jsx(_components.p,{children:`The parent’s cache is unaffected. `,_jsx(_components.a,{href:`/en/sub-agents#fork-the-current-conversation`,children:`fork`}),`, by contrast, inherits the parent’s system prompt, tools, and conversation history exactly, so its first
